<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which biological process best matches this protein’s direct nucleotide product-forming activity?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> dUTP biosynthetic process<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Thymidylate biosynthetic process<br><br><strong>C.</strong> De novo purine nucleotide biosynthetic process<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Pyrimidine nucleotide degradation<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>Analyzing the protein sequence, I can identify key motifs that are characteristic of **dUTPase (dUTP pyrophosphatase, EC 3.6.1.23)**:<br>- The &quot;RIDPGWSGCIVLEFY&quot; motif is a hallmark of dUTPase proteins<br>- The &quot;LIGALS&quot; motif further confirms dUTPase identity<br><br>dUTPase catalyzes the hydrolysis of dUTP (deoxyuridine triphosphate) to dUMP (deoxyuridine monophosphate) and pyrophosphate:<br>**dUTP + H₂O → dUMP + PPi**<br><br>Now evaluating each option based on the &quot;direct nucleotide product-forming activity&quot;:<br><br>- **A. dUTP biosynthetic process**: Incorrect. dUTPase *breaks down* dUTP, it doesn&#x27;t synthesize it.<br>- **B. Thymidylate biosynthetic process**: While dUMP feeds into thymidylate synthesis downstream, dUTPase does not directly form thymidylate (dTMP). The enzyme thymidylate synthase does that.<br>- **C. De novo purine nucleotide biosynthetic process**: Incorrect. This is a pyrimidine pathway enzyme, not purine.<br>- **D. Pyrimidine nucleotide degradation**: Correct. dUTP is a pyrimidine nucleotide, and its conversion to dUMP by dUTPase is a catabolic/degradation reaction of a pyrimidine nucleotide.
